FILE NOTE

1. At 1215 hrs we received a call from Section 40 | who vee
reported a "UFO" sighting to West Drayton earlier this week;

West Drayton told him to report the sighting to the MOD.

2. At 1705 hrs on Wednesday 21 December in Suffolk outside
his home he saw a very bright light which was at very high
altitude at high speed. It was pulsating and moving in a

zigzig manner; the night wa nd frosty and the
sighting lasted 5 minutes. brother also witnessed
the sighting.

3. I advised BERR of our interest in such sightings and
told him that if an explanation was forthcoming, that I would

let him know. ~~

FILE NOTE

1. [kat ted ap on 19 Dec 94 (pm).

2. On Wednesday 14 December at 1900 hours in the Hope Valley

saw a vivid green ball which hovered then flew away
in a dead straight line and there did not seem to_be a period
of acceleration. There was no apparent sound. said
that he is used to Tornado aircraft low flying in the area and
it definitely was not one of them. In relation to the apparent
lack of acceleration he thought that an ordinary aircraft could
not physically perform the manoeuvre this craft seemed to.

3. Contact Details:

Hope
Nr Sheffield

20 Dec 94

FILE NOTE

1. At 1500 hrs received phone call from BEG of
Forrest Hill. At 0330 hrs on 15 December a craft with 3 green
lights in a triangular formation hovered for 1-2 minutes over
his house. It made a loud humming/screeching noise. The craft
hovered at 300-400 ft in the air and then, rather than the
craft moving away he said the lights just seemed to go out.

The evening was very clear.

2. Did not accept that it could have been a helicopter. He
said he would know a helicopter, and besides he did not feel
the downdraft. I pointed out that he may not feel one if the
craft was 400 ft in the air. said his friend also
witnessed the event but he did not want his name revealed to

the MOD.

3. The most obvious possible explanation was that it may have
been a civil/police helicopter.

4. SECON details are as follows:

Forrest Hill
SE23

5. I said I would get in touch with him if an explanation was
forthcoming.

15 Dec 94

REPORTED "UFO" SIGHTING
Received a call from Flt Lt CRO Cottesmore
He had been contacted by the Coalville police about a reported
sighting in the Coalville area early Thursday mornin Section 40|
(14 December). Time of sighting 0130-0145 hrs, WPC was

driving and saw a bright blue vapour trail emitting from an
aircraft.

Cottesmore were closed and nothing was flying from there. The
local ATC could not account for the sighting; however, it could
have been other civil air traffic which was not flying in
controlled airspace.

Coalville Police Station: Section 40] |

Explained feeg Section 40] that unless were received any evidence to
substantiate the existence of a threat we would not be pursuing

the matter.

British Unidentified Flying Qbject Association
B.U.F.O.R.LA

Dear Madan,

-~ As an investigator with the above association I now find it
necessary tolmake an approach to the M.0.D. with regard to an unusual
event on tne (1743 of November 1994. :

The evert’ took place near Paxton village at about Spm and again
near Preston village at 6pm. These villages are both in Berwickshire.
I will now describe the object which at least four people saw that
night. The object was disc shaped and black in colour. Tt had two large
spot lights on either side of the dise with red and green lights which
were on constantly. There were other lights on the side of the cbject in
particular a small but very bright light which again was on all the time.
This object had the capability of hovering fifty feet above the ground in
total silence and was at least four times the size of a Tornado fighter
jet, at approximately two hundred yards away. _ wane

The second event took place on the Ctwenty second of November 1994
at about 7pm. This object was of a different design. It was triangular
with sharp edges, being black at one time during the sighting then changed
to a dark red. It came slowly in from the North sea at a height of 20 feet
and up the estuary of the river Tweed. It then stopped, hovered, turned a
dark red and lit the water underneath, a bright red colour. Again, this
object was silent. Having hovered for approximately 3 minutes it shot off
at an incredible speed passing underneath ail three bridges which span the
river.

I would’ now like to ask the M.O.D. aif there were any aircraft in
hese areas which could fit the descriptions given above? I alse wish to
ask if there were any unexplained blips on the R.A.D.A.R installation at
Boulmer on these nights or any other early warning system on the east
coast of the U.K? We at B.U.F.O.R.A would be very grateful for any

information no matter how small you care to give us.
Thank you for taking the time and trouble to read this letter and IT would
like to wish you all a very merry Xmas and a happy New Year.

Yours Faithfully,

REPORT FROM SUFFOLK POLICE

PC rom Suffolk Police called this afternoon at 3 ish to

forward details of a reported "UFO" sighting from the
following:

Stoke-by-—Clare
Suffolk

At 0425 hrs on Sun 11 Dec he saw a very bright white light

hovering stationary in the sky 3 miles away in the Clare area.
The sighting went on for 25 minutes.

DETAILS OF OCCURRENCE

POSS UFO SIGHTING

1. A possible UFO sighting was reported to Derbyshire Police Co at

2050hrs by;

Hope

Sana

2. Incident occurred near the A47, Snake Rd, north of Lady Bower Damm.

SESE -eported seeing a large orange light moving horizontally and
dropping slowly until it went below a line of hills. A cloud of smoke was

seen to billow out from the light and it then disappeared behind Kinder
Scout. The light did not reappear.

3. No other sightings reported.

ACTION TAKEN

1. Derbyshire Police contacted Manchester Airport ATC who couldn't confirm
any aicraft were operating in the area and could offer no explanation for

the sighting.

FILE NOTE - Reported Sighting

The Suffolk Police called at about 0900 hrs. AfERMeMAOM
called at 0815 hrs this morning. He and three other witnesses
saw a very large round object which was "red and hot looking".
There was a bright light under the object and it looked as
though it was "covered in cling film". There were 4 bright
lights near it.

The sighting lasted 10 minutes, and was seen with naked
eyes. After 10 minutes it soared up into the sky to about
"75,000 feet".

Sighting occurred over Stowmarket, Suffolk, which is east
of Bury St Edmonds. The police officer said that this place is
very close to Wattisham. They (the police) contacted Wattisham
and Wattisham were not aware of any unusual radar traces.

FILE NOTE

Reported Sighting - 1005 hrs Mon 5 Dec

Hutton
Brentwood
Essex

At 0515 hrs this morning until 0740 hrs saw a very bright
light, bigger and brighter than a star. No noise with a tiny
ved light under the base. Her neighbour said he thought it was
a weather balloon. She ruled it out as a helicopter because
she could hear no sound. She could not say how far it was away
from her.

I explained the MOD's interest. She wanted to get in touch
with BUFORA so I offered her the telephone number.

Station: Clacton on Sea

Subject: REPORT OF U.F.O. SIGHTING

Date: 28 November, (994

To The DUTY INSPECTOR
{ have to report with reference to Standing order 36 paragraph 81
A. 28th November 1994 1700hrs for approx. 3 - 4 minutes

B. one unidentified flying object described as triangular in shape and of very large size, no distinguishable
colours, having two large bright white lights and the object being surrounded by pulsating red lights.
described as being silent.

C. firstly observed by informant in his car MSection40 FE continued to be observed in the

informants garden of his home address at Clacton on sea, Essex

D. observed with the naked eye.

E. Towards the direction of Thorpe le soken.

F, approximately 45 degrees from ground

G. described as being about aeroplane normal flying distance away

H. described as cruising speed towards the general direction of Thorpe and Colchester.
J. clear night sky

informant states there was an aeroplane in his sight of vision at the time of observation which he claims
was higher in the sky than the ufo. but travelling in another direction. There were no other objects nearby
to interfere with the observation.

L.._ reported to Clacton Police control, PaSection 40|
, Se

N. None known.

O.  Informants mother also witnessed the ever of the same address

P. Report received at 171 1hrs 28.11.94

Lask that this report be forwarded for the attention of A.C.C.(A) for onward transmission to
Ministry of Defence, Air Force Operations

Main eeesion Oils
London SW1A 2HB

Police Sergeant eon 40

‘Station: -Clacton on Sea

Subject: Report of U.F.O. Sighting

Date: 28 November, 1994

am A357
v. 979
L AlT3

To the Divisional Commander

The attached report from Ps| @ submitted in accordance with Standing Order 36/81.

It will be seen from the IRIS incident’ 736 of 28 119. i observers, Police

Officers included. The crew of| elt that they were

watching alow g.aircrar saw an object from a moving vehicle on The Avenue of
Remembrance at Colchester. There is no way of telling if the officers are describing the same object. Bion 40]
reported seeing an object at Thorpe le Soken, Essex, which

apparently ties in with the account from the original informant. I have been unable to recontact

to question her in detail about direction of flight, angle of viewing etc, as the only reply from her home is an
answerphone.

No enquiries have been made from this station with any establishments who may have had aircraft in the area at
the time of the sightings, which may offer explanations of what was actually seen.

Cask that these reports be forwarded via ACC(A), in accordance with Standing Orders, to the Ministry of
Defence.

Inspector.

E 21st November 1994%

3. WIA

Dear Sir,

Acting on the advice eamSection40 | author, I am writing
to tell you of a sighting I had last week which he said would
possibly interest you.

My occupation is that of EOE and Tam _ currentl
seconded to the Prison Service. I work in the Section 40.
On Tuesday, 15th November, I was in the Probation General

Office which is located in the administration building known

as The Facade. Norwich Prison occupies a position on Mousehold
Heath about a hundred feet above the level of the City. The
pbuilding faces W.S.W. directly towards the C.E.Cathedral.

At approximately 4.15 p.m. a member of the Support Staff asked
me whether I could identify a row of bright lights to the West.
Together we looked at a map of the City and concluded that the
lights were on the ground in the vicinity of the University.
They appeared to be the kind of lights used on a sports ground.
My colleague had not noticed them before.

The next day, Wednesday, I was in the office, the time being
approximately 7.45 a.m. I looked once again at the map of the
City and having done so went to the window to see whether I
could identify where it was that we had seen the lights on the
previous day.

The morning was exceptionally bright, the sky clear of cloud.
The air temperature was mild, there was little, if any, breeze.
There was no haze over the City.

While I was searching the sky-line to the right, or W.N.W., my
eyes were made conscious of a light to my left, two lights in
fact. I enclose a sketch I made a couple of minutes later which
will give an idea of the position of the images in relation to
the spire of the Cathedral. The sketch is made sight-size.

At first I thought the scene was ordinary enough; the rising
sun was already strong and it seemed that I was seeing two hot
air balloons, formed from a silvery material. They were identical
in size and perfectly round, about 1/4" to 3/8" diameter.

They appeared solid, faced directly to the sun I neither
expected to see nor did I see any lessening of reflected

light to the edges of the images and so they appeared not. to
be spherical. Neither of the images showed any movement during
the minute or so that I observed them.

I noticed that there were no baskets beneath the balloons and
although they appeared to have little altitude I saw no bursts
of flame as I should have expected. The brightness of the
light coming from the surfaces of the objects made it
impossible for me to estimate their distance.

There was neither sight nor sound of aircraft, neither was
there, after the images ceased to be visible, any cloud or
indication of their presence. The objects appeared to be
substantial, to be reflecting light and not emanating it.

Without the objects having moved in relation to the buildings
beneath them, after about’ a minute they both vanished in an
instant; they did diminish in intensity of light or size but
vanished from view.™ yy,

The windows to the office had been cleaned two or three days
before the sighting. There was no light on in the office and
neither was any of the office equipment switched on. What I

saw was not a reflection on the window from within the room.

The sketch was made immediately after I saw the images and
drawn from the same position in which I had stood. At no time
while watching the images or objects did they make any kind of
movement.

If I am able to help you in any way in connection with the
matter described will you please contact me. If you need to do
so by telephone please will you do so after 7.00 p.m.

Yours faithfully,

FILE NOTE

Contacted by Misection 40) Last night at about 1900 hrs his
Secon AO a nephew saw a 60 foot object 200 feet above the
ground in Romford, Essex. There was lights which were rotating
in an alternating fashion. The evening was a foggy but still

one. The object made no noise hovered then dramatically sped
upwards and out of sight.

I have had no other reporting sightings in the Romford area. I
undertook to get back to him if it became apparent what it was.

7 Nov 94

REPORTED UAP SIGHTING

called me this morning at approx 0900 hrs to say
that she and her boyfriend were out driving in
their car between Bently, Odiham and Reading (down country
lanes) on Saturday evening at 1800 hrs when they saw a sighting
30 feet up in the air at an angle of 45 degrees 30 feet away
from them.

It followed their car for 10 miles and was made up of 3 circles
of light which seemed to be surrounded by clouds. There was no
sound and the shape rotated anti-clockwise.

vening was clear and the sighting occurred over fields.
Secion 40, AES certain it was not a hovering helio or any
aircraft.

I explained our limited role and said I would let her know if
anyone else reported the sighting, especially if it revealed an
explanation to put her mind at rest.

About half an hour Speers section 40 | rang back as she was
concerned that ufos might emit radiation and were she and her
boyfriend at risk. 1 told her that the MOD did not have
evidence to support the existence of spacecraft of extra—
terrestrial origin and therefore it was highly unlikely that

they were at risk from radiation.

Contact Details:

Farnham
surrey

3 Nov 94

"UFO" REPORTS FROM RAF LEUCHARS

1. Unusually, in the space of 4 days we received 5 “ufo"
reports via RAF Leuchars. They were spread across a very wide
area. I spoke to the Community Relations Officer at Leuchars,

Flight Lieutenant who in the first instance was
not aware that th ad been sent to us.
2. I asked if there was any unusual exercise, or night flying

taking place, and he said there wasn't. He had not seen any
reports in the Press either, and they were usually pretty hot
on picking things like this up.

3. He was content that there was not any untoward activity
taking place in the area.

L.. Flight Lieutenant GEMM@Mnas passed me all of the

information of your report of 4 of the sighting of a possi
“snidentified flying object. The information has now been
 pagsed on to offices at the Ministry of Defence which examines
“grb such reports. However, their task is limited to checking
for @ polential threat to the security and defence of the
United Kingdom, You may be contacted if they require further

a

fornation.

ae fhe direction of the event suggests that this may have
been a ataged event in either Leeds or Dewsbury, especially
wren the start time is considered. It could have been either
a firework or laser digplay, probably the latter. The
descending light at the end of the display suggests that it
“could have. been a circling helicopter but such an effect can
be created in laser shows. ‘he fact that there were no stars
and that rain fell during the event suggests that low clouds
could have been present, which would have made 4 laser show
even more spectacular. The orange colouring of the sky and
the distant sounds could have been enhenced by the mixing of
“£€ireworks with the light show. A search of the weekend's
jocal papers may find a suitable source.

“4. . Thank you for your report and I hope that Ll have set. your
mind at rest with a possible solution. . .

Flight. Lieutenant

ROYAL AIR FORCE
Coitishall Norwich Norfolk NR10 5AJ

Your reference

Our reference

COLT/1466/4/0Org
Date

ROoctober 1994

Thank you for your report to RAF Coltishall concerning a sighting at night by three

young ladies of two unidentified flying objects.

Accordingly, I have sent the report to the Ministry of Defence, Main Building,

Whitehall, London. The Ministry of Defence does not investigate sightings except

to establish any defence interest. However, if the phenomenon spotted by your .
daughter and her friends is a known one, a reply will be forwarded.

Last,. please pass on my thanks to the youngsters concerned for a very clear picture
painted of the phenomenon under what were frightening circumstances to them.

Copy To:- | .

Dear BEM 40

Thanks . for the SERIE 122 info! it looks like a | good
casé¢, despite the location of the witnesses at the time! :

; Section 40) and SERIE bo. ‘submitted very lucid, reports,

obviously written nr ependentiy. It's my impression. that.
they . were probably standing towards the -rear of. that
lastonbury Festival concert audience at the time of. the

sighting. It's not too surprising that no one else paid much —
attention,to « happening overhead in those circumstances. :

In. their’ diagrams the witnesses have depicted the direction
of the object’s circling motion differently, but then one
can't be’ clear about the viewpoint taken. by each. ‘It's

interesting that remembers the coloured lights .
displayed. by the object to have been square, whereas’ no
mention of this is made by her companion. (Such differences

in reports could be dependent on the relative sharpness — of»
the eyesight of the witnesses —-~ -and your current.
questionnaire doesn't ask whether glasses are usually worn
or were being worn).

The timing of the event is excellent, beginning only 6:
minutes after a predicted delivery from.a sunset-orientated
track, 54-degrees inclined. (See attached numerical comput

output sheet). ‘ : oan
By the way, I've recently taken the time to review: my.
collection of those computer-processed runs which ‘resulted™
in positive correlations with the Astronautical Model (which
accounts, in part, for the slowness ofthis response from
me). They cover the period 1950 to present day andthere are

342 in total. 317 are British. Using my sort program, I've.
been able to identify. the ground tracks most | commonly
followed by the hypothetical delivery/retrieval vehicles
during that period of time. As you. will’ see from “the,
attached map, there are 26 of. them -- ignoring those shown

but not listed; but the favoured ground tracks are’. linked
with only 9 of the possible 21 ‘orbit’ ‘options’ covering:

British latitudes. Together, these tracks relate 9
(92.4%) of the viable sightings. : : a
The Glastonbury happening relates (time-wise © and

geographically) to the tracks labelled 54°/65°E (delivery)
and 58°/51°E (retrieval) on the map. a ea
‘Where tracks converge it could be expected that such
locations might have the characteristics of ‘ufocals'. Of
special ‘interest (in view of the Sturminster. Marshall,
Dorset, LITS event of 9.8.93) is the convergence of ‘tracks
north of Poole. ‘Of special interest, also, is that they've
got London surrounded! My various evaluations of London
events in which I’ve identified the tracks used should. make
more sense to you now Have I convinced you yet that we
really are dealing wi an astronautical activity? :
Section 40] Best wishes, :

LETTER FRO gsection 4§ pec 40

1. Further to teleconEE@M MM earlier today, I attach as
‘promised the letter from the above initially passed to us by
APS/S of S for reply that we discussed. ; :

2. As. you can.see, the writer touches on a wide range of issues
some ‘of which might be seen as "scientific" eg. anti-gravity,
flying saucers, etc.. Clearly however the main thrust of his
letter is his desire for a job and what appears to his belief
that "C. Summers"(?) has rejected his application for a “career
in the Security Services". ;

3. As discussed therefore it seems more appropriate for CM to
lead in replying. =.

4. For the record, we are not aware of any research into "“anti-
gravity" at British Aerospace Preston or elsewhere. As for “the
enigma of the so called flying saucers" possibly Sec(AS)2a, to

whom I have copied this correspondence, may have some views.

Sot

2. I should be grateful if you would arrange for the appropriate
action to be taken, including a reply as necessary. Since the
Secretary of State attaches importance to such letters addressed to
him being answered promptly, any reply should be sent within two
weeks of the date of this minute. If, exceptionally, this should
prove impossible an interim reply should be sent within the same

timescale.

3. The Open Government Code of Practice came into force on 4th
April 1994. You should ensure-that all replies to members of the
public are provided in accordance with the procedures as set out in
the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained

in pct(Gen) 112/94; further information is available from

LONDON.

Dear Mr. Rifkind.

T recently wrote to:
The C.V. was simple and to the point, 1 was applying after
£72,000 of education to go into my well prepared for and well
sought after career in the Security Services.

the point is Mr. Rifking they have said there ar
vacancies, with all that education and knowledge I clean some
ones house and mow lawns for less than two pounds fifty an hour.

The point I am making to you is simple why has all £72,000 of
education been paid for by Lancashire County Council and when

lL reach fruition I can not put my self to working for the society
that has paid for me for the. last twenty two years.

How do you feel that £72,000 of your tax money has gone into educating
myself and now I am not allowed to get some work done?

I have offered to do something for them for free to show

them that I am legitimate in my yearn to work in National Security
they can't even take the trouble to send me a security pass to

do some research for them.

Will you try to resove this. dispute, i.e. that they will at Jeast
give me a contact and an interview so that I may be of some value
to the society, after all L have been wanting to do the job for
twelve years and worked hard. ;

I take this opportunity to congratulate you on making the changes
you did recently in reducing the size and capability of our defences;
this will pave the way for a better, safer future, where peace

will be the watch word and not. war.

Your duty to our country is very demanding at best and I
sympathise with you that often progress is difficult to forge,
but for those who are willing to work for our National Security
through a sense of loyalty and patriotism please let them do SOs.

I further, take this chance to wish you every success in imple-
menting further changes to radically reform our defence, for a
long time things have been running “unefficiently" with many people
chasing paper in the MOD and you have weeded them out, good on

you.
Second Page, Final Page.

I wish there was more I could do for my country but if the Security
Services won't even interview people before passing judgment,

then I can't forsee that our projects of a sensitive nature will
ever be secure. Too many secrets find their way under the door,

I don't like that and I deerly would like to do something about

it; sadly the people who could give me the opportunity are being

difficult.

The research that I would like to access at British Aerospace

at Preston in Lancashire concerns anti-gravity. The application
could be made practical within the next thirty years, and I believe
we may solve the enigma of the so-called fling saucers simultaneosly.

You have always. been a hard worker Mr. Rifkind, and so.you can.

appreciate that it-greaves me that I have worked -hard and-can't
seem to embark on the career I am destined for.

I am your obedient servant,

INVOLVEMENT OF THE BELGIAN AIR FORCE

I thought it would be worthwhile to describe briefly the soci
context before going on to discuss the involvement of the Ad
Force in the debate on UFOs. ;

When I was asked to attend the SOBEPS press conference on 18
December 1989, as Chief of Operations of the Air Staff, I was
sceptical about the existence of UFOs. Having said that, I was

determined to approach the problem without prejudice, as far as

possible, and to examine it in an objective manner. Two factors
became apparent to me during this press conference. Firstly, the

evidence was remarkable. It was not frivolous and was presented

in a natural and rather modest manner: no trace of sensationalism

or exaggerated media "hype". Secondly, the approach of SOBEPS was

sober, objective and based on scientific facts. Evidence which

could be linked to natural phenomena was rejected immediately.

Another important factor: certain journalists had the armed

forces as their target, in particular the Air Force. The

phenomena observed were alleged to have been experimental

aircraft, the existence of which was to be concealed from the

public.

Some saw a link with the F-117 and used the opportunity to
eriticise American "imperialism". The alleged servility of the
Belgian authorities, who had, consciously or not, allowed such
experiments, was also denounced. Moreover, there were rumours to
the effect that observations of certain UFOs had been made by
military radars, about which no information could be disclosed.

Initially, the Air Force was obliged to deny the hypothesis of
experimental test flights. This was fairly simple, and the
context is amply described’ in the previous chapters. The Air
Force could put its cards. on the table for the simple reason that
there was nothing to hide, and that it was impossible from a
technical point of view that an F-117 could have been involved.

In addition, the seriousness of the evidence and the
professionalism of SOBEPS were factors which prompted the Air
Force to decide on the need for a more in-depth study. This is
why it had decided before the holding of the press conference to
try to identify the nature and origin of certain observed:
phenomena. The big question was, however, with what means?

THE MEANS

Every flight in Belgian airspace carried out between the setting
and rising of ‘the sun by any aircraft whatsoever must be the
subject of an official request and must receive clearance from
the civil and/or military authorities. These two bodies are
responsible for coordinating all air movements during the night
so that the identity of all aircraft in flight is known, together
with their intentions, the aim being, of course, to ensure the
safety of air navigation. In the case of the UFO observations,
it was therefore a case of checking whether any non-regulation
flight had been carried out. If this were indeed found to be the
case, it goes without saying that we would have. to try to
identify these aircraft.
One of the most obvious means of detecting intrusions is radar.
In addition to the approach radars of the various airfields, the
Aix Force has two powerful detection radars integrated into the
NATO defence system and operational round the clock. The
detection range of these radars is in excess of 300 km. Aircraft
can be detected up to an altitude of approximately 30 km, whilst
the minimum altitude depends on their position and the distance
between them and the station. This goes without saying when one
xealizes that radar waves cannot see round corners and that each
target hidden behind the horizon line or behind an obstacle (a
hill, for example) cannot generally be detected.

In normal circumstances, the radar controller sees only the
aircraft which have already undergone a selection procedure. In
fact, a computer analyses the reflected waves and will reject all
echoes which do not meet the preprogrammed standards (for
example, the flight envelope of an aircraft). The controller
therefore sees only the synthesized image of the radar echoes
which in all probability represent aircraft.

In this way, spurious signals are eliminated. The latter, better
the controllers as "angels" (see contribution of BRIE.
Becton 40 | can be caused by atmospheric disturbance, for example —
ayer of atmospheric inversion which deflects radar waves

which, in their turn, are reflected by, among other things, by
moving objects on the ground.

The controller can switch off the computer filtering so that he
can observe on the screen all the echoes, including the "angels"
(so-called "raw video" mode). It should be added immediately that
the radar-operators have little experience of this way of working
and there are many echoes of ‘which the origin is unknown.

In the case of UFO observations, the radar controllers were asked
to pay particular attention to the echoes (even on "raw video")
which appeared in the region of the visual observations on the
ground. Thus, a number of radar echoes were recorded where it was
impossible to know whether they were "angels" or real aircraft
in flight. Hence the rather cautious attitude of the Air Force,
particularly at the beginning of the period of observation and
the resulting criticism that certain information could not be
disclosed.

Apart from these radar stations, the Air Force also has an
Interception Wing equipped with F-16 combat aircraft at
Beauvechain, approximately 30 km south-east of Brussels. Two of
these F-16s are permanently ready to take off within five
minutes, following an order given by a. duty officer within the
NATO defence system. The duty radar controller can, if necessary,
obtain such authorization within a very short time. The condition
‘is that a concrete radar observation of unknown origin occurs and
that he requests visual identification, using the two F-16 ready
for immediate take-off. This procedure is the subject of regular
exercises: the pilots are from time to time sent up to identify
presumed intrusions, simulated or not. The purpose is to rehearse
all the procedures in depth so that, in case of need, an urgent
intervention can take place without problems.
After the first wave of observations on 29 November 1989, the Air
Force instructed the radar controllers and the combat pilots to
be vigilant and if necessary to initiate an identification
procedure. For this purpose, only the existing resources, i.e.
two ground radars and the F~16s, were to be used. The aim was to
check whether the UFOs were real or not. After confirmation, we
would move to the second stage, identification and determining
whether the safety of air traffic was threatened.

ACTIONS

Coordination between the ground observations and the radar
control centre of Glons posed, in this context, the greatest
problem.

The Gendarmerie is the organization one immediately thought of
to carry out this task. To this end, the telephone number of the
radar station of Glons was passed to the gendarmerie brigades
concerned so that they could inform the controllers of the
position of the geographical positions of certain objects
observed.

The number of links in the chain of communication between the
observers on the ground and the pilots whose task it is to
identify any flying objects represented a major handicap
(telephone call to the gendarmes, patrol in position, radio link
with the brigade, call to the Glons centre, telephone call to the
controller and.radio.link with the pilots in flight).

Like the Air Force, the Gendarmerie was unable to deploy any
additional resources to carry out its mission. Quite logically,
priority was given to the traditional tasks assigned to the few
gendarmes on duty in the evening and at night, resulting in a not
inconsiderable loss of time, since it was impossible t6 react
promptly to specific calls at a specific time.

The Air Force carried out a number of concrete actions. In a
first action on the evening of 2 December, two F-16s were sent
to the Liége area where a possible sighting had taken place. A
telephone call from the Gendarmerie informed the duty controller
of a ground observation; the latter noted at the position
indicated a radar echo (on raw video) which possibly confirmed
the visual observation. The F-16s were dispatched to the
position, saw nothing and returned to their base. According to
the radar controller, the echo disappeared when the F-16s
approached the area and reappeared after their return to base.

The mystery remains unresolved.

Two aircraft were sent on a second mission following innumerable
telephone calls from the Hasselt area. The ground radars had seen
nothing and the F~16s were able to identify the origin of the
phenomenon as laser projections onto a cloud layer. Analysis of
the facts has shown that these observations were completely
different from those made earlier in the province of Liége.

After this incident, orders were given not to dispatch aircraft
without previous radar observation and without formal
. confirmation of the ground observations by the Gendarmerie. ~

Then. there was the action of the night of 30 to 31 March 1990
_ degeribed in this book.

In this | case, “the Air Force reacted only after telephone
confirmation from the Gendarmerie of a number of observations in
the Wavre region and after simultaneous observation by two ground
- yadars. The F-16s. carried out several interceptions with the aid

of directions from the duty radar controller. Certain data were
recorded by the airborne.radars of the F-16s; their technical

analysis. is. described : as contribution to this |

book.
The ‘Air Force itself. approached the affair withthe greatest

caution. Electromagnetic interference which can:lead to false. .

conclusions is not ‘unusual both for airborne and ground radars. .
Interference on airborne radars is however generally caused by

ground conditions whereas the recordings on 30/31 March were made
at an altitude of approximately 3 km.. Moreover, a correlation was
noted at certain times between the data originating from the. two
airborne radars and at least one ground radar. The recording of
identical data by three different systems at given times’ means
that we cannot rule out the possibility that one or more
unidentified aircraft were indeed operating in Belgian airspace.

It shouldbe said immediately that this was the first time that™
an in-depth: analysis had been. carried out. of data completely.
outside the area of known performance in the aeronautical field.
Was it really. a question of aircraft or of exceptional
“electromagnetic: interference? :

The pilots themselves. had no concrete visual contacts, which are”.
in’ fact very difficult to obtain in view of the incalculable

number of points. of light visible at night over Belgium with a
.eloudless sky and good visibility. However, when a pilot locks

on-his radar to a. possible target, an. indication of the place
where it should be located is. displayed on his screen. Nothing.

was recorded by the pilots during the various lock-ons carried.
out. If these targets. had been: illuminated, this would certainly
shave-been the case. Is this proof that there was nothing in the
air and that it was a matter of electromagnetic interference?
Certainly not: eye-witnesses at Petit-Rosiére stated that the —
bright spots they were observing lost their intensity asthe F~.

16s approached or were even extinguished completely. ;

Why was no definite correlation found between the. ground
observations at Ramillies and the recordings of the airborne
radars of the F-16s?

According to eyewitnesses, the luminous. objects sometimes
travelled in jerky movements, subsequently stabilizing at a
determined geographical position.

But in its interception function, the radar of an F-16: cannot
detect a static object, for the simple reason that the system.
rejects all.echoes of objects moving at relative speeds of less oe
than +/- 100°km. P- h. ;

,*

This filtering was incorporated into the system to avoid the
airborne radar detecting all the cars or other objects on the
‘ground and therefore’ to prevent the screen from being cluttered
by too large a number of echoes.

The pilot is interested only in aircraft and helicopters moving
at higher speeds. These latter targets will be the only ones to
be projected onto the screen, enabling the pilot to see the
flight profile and, if necessary, to carry out an interception.

From a technical point of view, it was therefore impossible to
detect luminous objects with the airborne radar if they remained
immobile or moved at low speeds.

At all events, the Air Force has arrived at the conclusion that
a number of abnormal phenomena have occurred in Belgian airspace.

The extensive evidence of ground observations collected in this
book, reinforced by the recordings of the night of 30/31 March,
have led us to consider the hypothesis that a number of
unauthorized air activities have indeed taken place.

So far, no trace of aggression has been reported; military and
civil air traffic has been neither disrupted nor threatened. It
can therefore be advanced that the alleged air activities
constituted no concrete threat.

Since the armed forces react. only in the event of a potential
threat, it was. decided not to use additional resources or
personnel beyond the existing and available air defence systems.
I believe that as regards the ground. observations, the
Gendarmerie has arrived at’ an identical conclusion.

LS2/1905/KDM

for a ten minute ‘period.

Dosediption of Object. 3 large objects. Leading object had
flashing red and white lights. The second object was white
and the third object (largest) was orange and left an orange
trail.

Exact Position Observer. Outside house, in garden.

“How Observed. Normal vision.

Direction in-which object was first seen. Travelling toward
Herringthorpe.. :

Angle of Sight. Looking up at a 45 angle.
Distance. Approximately 1.5 miles at 1000 feet.
Movements. Objects 1 &.2 stationary for approximately 3

seconds. Then re-commenced ‘movement. Object 3 travelled in
a straight’ line.

Meteorological conditions during observations. Clear sky.
